In practice, people usually start with the general mechanism, then adjust for the specifics they can observe. If you want, I can go deeper on any one of these.

T	What causes coral bleaching?	There are a few ways to look at this. The most common explanation focuses on the main driver and treats everything else as a correction. A second view emphasizes the boundary conditions, where the simple story breaks down. A third is purely practical and ignores the theory, relying instead on what has worked before. None of these is wrong; they answer slightly different questions. For most everyday purposes the practical view is enough, but the mechanism view helps when something unusual happens.

T	Why do tides vary through the month?	Good question. The short version is that it depends on several factors that interact. First, the underlying mechanism sets the broad shape of the answer. Second, local conditions shift the details, which is why simple rules of thumb often fail. Third, timing matters: the same inputs can give different outcomes depending on when they occur. In practice, people usually start with the general mechanism, then adjust for the specifics they can observe. If you want, I can go deeper on any one of these.

None of these is wrong; they answer slightly different questions. For most everyday purposes the practical view is enough, but the mechanism view helps when something unusual happens.
T	How should I maintain a sea kayak?	It helps to separate two things: what is happening and why it matters. What is happening is fairly well understood and can be described in a few sentences. Why it matters depends on your goals, and reasonable people differ there. A useful habit is to state the mechanism first, then the consequence, then the uncertainty. That ordering keeps the discussion honest and makes it obvious where the disagreement actually lies.

T	What should I look for in a used road bike?	There are a few ways to look at this. The most common explanation focuses on the main driver and treats everything else as a correction. A second view emphasizes the boundary conditions, where the simple story breaks down. A third is purely practical and ignores the theory, relying instead on what has worked before. None of these is wrong; they answer slightly different questions. For most everyday purposes the practical view is enough, but the mechanism view helps when something unusual happens.

T	Give me a short summary of the history of the bicycle.	There are a few ways to look at this. The most common explanation focuses on the main driver and treats everything else as a correction. A second view emphasizes the boundary conditions, where the simple story breaks down. A third is purely practical and ignores the theory, relying instead on what has worked before. None of these is wrong; they answer slightly different questions. For most everyday purposes the practical view is enough, but the mechanism view helps when something unusual happens.
